PD Disaggregation - SGLang Documentation 

 Documentation Index Fetch the complete documentation index at: /llms.txt Use this file to discover all available pages before exploring further. Skip to main content Assistant Responses are generated using AI and may contain mistakes. ​ Why and What is PD Disaggregation? 
 Large Language Model (LLM) inference comprises two distinct phases: Prefill and Decode . The Prefill phase is computation-intensive, processing the entire input sequence, while the Decode phase is memory-intensive, managing the Key-Value (KV) cache for token generation. Traditionally, these phases are handled within a unified engine, where combined scheduling of prefill and decode batches introduces inefficiencies. To address these challenges, we introduce Prefill and Decoding (PD) Disaggregation in SGLang. 
 ​ Issues with Unified Scheduling 
 The conventional unified engine, which processes prefill and decode batches together, results in two significant problems: 

 Prefill Interruption : Incoming prefill batches frequently interrupt ongoing decode batches, causing substantial delays in token generation. 
 DP Attention Imbalance : In data-parallel (DP) attention, one DP worker may process a prefill batch while another handles a decode batch simultaneously, leading to increased decode latency. 

 PD Disaggregation resolves these by separating the two stages, enabling tailored optimizations for each. 
 For the design details, please refer to link . 
 Currently, we support Mooncake and NIXL as the transfer engine. 
 ​ Decode allocation policy 
 By default, decode reserves each request’s destination KV slots before prefill
finishes. Long prefills can hold these slots while decode waits for the KV data.
To delay this reservation, set the following flag on both prefill and decode : 
 --disaggregation-decode-allocation-policy prefill_complete 

 With prefill_complete
, prefill computes without waiting for destination slots,
then signals readiness after accepting its final prefill result. Decode reserves
slots and starts the existing KV transfer handshake only after that signal.
This changes per-request KV reservation, not the size or creation of the global
KV pool. The default early
 policy preserves the existing behavior. 
 Deferring allocation can help when long prefills compete with active decode
requests for destination KV capacity. It also retains KV on prefill longer and
removes overlap between chunked prefill and KV transfer. Compare both policies
for your workload; this option is not a general throughput improvement. 
 This initial implementation requires Mooncake (RDMA or mooncake_tcp
), the Python
bootstrap registry, and attention TP/CP and pipeline sizes of one. Attention DP
is allowed when each DP rank has attention TP size one. Prefill automatically
resolves --optimistic-prefill-attempts
 from zero to one; configurations that
disable optimistic prefill after model validation are rejected.
Chunked prefill does not yield just because decode has not admitted the request.
Completed requests retain their KV in the existing optimistic-prefill inflight
queue until decode supplies destination indices; readiness alone never starts
a KV write.
After a successful transfer, prefill clears the room’s readiness and transfer
state before notifying decode of success. Retraction recovery can then reuse
the same bootstrap room. This notification waits for worker and scheduler
cleanup; failure and cancellation records remain until their timeout expires.
Retries remain eligible for prefill after the optimistic attempt budget is
exhausted and remain subject to the existing cancellation and timeout handling. 
 On prefill, SGLANG_DISAGGREGATION_BOOTSTRAP_TIMEOUT
 starts after the final
prefill result, giving decode admission its own budget. On decode,
 SGLANG_DISAGGREGATION_WAITING_TIMEOUT
 bounds the wait for prefill completion,
then starts afresh for admission and again when destination indices are sent
for transfer. 
 The option does not support staging buffers, runtime PD role switching,
encoder disaggregation, decoupled speculation, HiSparse, decode radix cache, decode
HiCache, or non-DSA auxiliary state pools. Prefill HiCache is limited to L2 with
 write_back
 and no storage backend. Unsupported configurations fail validation. 
 All communicating workers must use the same revision and enable the same policy. This
version of the decode worker rejects policy or peer-layout mismatches before
destination registration. Older decode workers do not enforce this check. Keep
 early
 on both sides until every worker has been upgraded, then enable the new
policy on both sides. 
 ​ Profiling in PD Disaggregation Mode 
 When you need to profile prefill or decode workers in PD disaggregation mode, please refer to the Profile In PD Disaggregation Mode section in the Benchmark and Profiling guide. Due to torch profiler limitations, prefill and decode workers must be profiled separately using dedicated command-line options. 
 ​ Router Integration 
 For deploying PD disaggregation at scale with load balancing and fault tolerance, SGLang provides a router. The router can distribute requests between prefill and decode instances using various routing policies. For detailed information on setting up routing with PD disaggregation, including configuration options and deployment patterns, see the SGLang Model Gateway (former Router) . 
 ​ Responses API under HTTP PD 
 HTTP PD supports foreground generation through POST /v1/responses
, including streaming generation with stream=true
 through the HTTP PD router. This requires both the router’s Responses dispatch support and serving workers that accept Responses PD routing metadata. 
 Store-backed and stateful Responses features are not supported under PD : 

 Response persistence ( store
) and response history. 
 Conversation chaining with previous_response_id
. 
 Background Responses ( background=true
), including background streams. 
 Retrieval by response ID ( GET /v1/responses/{id}
). 
 Cancel-by-ID ( POST /v1/responses/{id}/cancel
). 
 Other workflows requiring shared process-local Responses history. 

 Prefill and decode run in separate serving processes. Their process-local Responses stores cannot provide coherent shared state: both legs need the same conversation history to reconstruct the prompt, and prefill’s stored response is not available to decode. Send explicit conversation history in foreground requests instead of relying on stored response IDs. 
 Built-in tools ( web_search
, code_interpreter
) are also unsupported under PD: each tool call requires another generation, but the router dispatches exactly one prefill/decode pair per HTTP request. 
 Serving-side capability enforcement is tracked in #39122 . That change introduces opt-in standalone storage with --enable-response-store
, disallows it under PD, and rejects stateful requests with HTTP 400 before generation. These checks require a serving version containing that change; older versions must not be assumed to reject every unsupported workflow at admission. The HTTP PD router also rejects detached background requests because it does not implement their retrieval/cancel lifecycle. 
 ​ Mooncake 
 ​ Requirements 
 uv pip install mooncake-transfer-engine 

 On Intel XPU, the PyPI wheel has no XPU support. Use the XPU Docker image ( docker/xpu.Dockerfile
), or build Mooncake from source with icpx and -DUSE_XPU=ON -DUSE_TENT=ON
; see Intel GPUs (XPU) for the runtime settings. 
 ​ AWS EFA 
 ​ EFA Docker images 
 Each published CUDA 13 image has an EFA variant for EFA-enabled AWS EC2 instances, built from the same SGLang commit with docker/efa.Dockerfile
: 
 Base image | EFA image | 
 latest
, v<version>
 | latest-efa
, v<version>-efa
 | 
 latest-runtime
, v<version>-runtime
 | latest-runtime-efa
, v<version>-runtime-efa
 | 
 nightly-dev-<date>-<sha>
 | nightly-dev-efa-<date>-<sha>
, plus the moving dev-efa
 | 

 Each EFA image matches its base and adds: 

 The AWS EFA userspace stack: libfabric with the EFA provider, the aws-ofi-nccl plugin, and hwloc. The host does not need its own EFA userspace install. 
 On x86_64, the EFA build of Mooncake with MOONCAKE_PROTOCOL=efa
. On arm64 the standard Mooncake package is kept and MOONCAKE_PROTOCOL=tcp
 is set, because the EFA build is x86_64-only. 
 SGLANG_DISAGGREGATION_NIXL_BACKEND=LIBFABRIC
, so NIXL transfers use EFA on both architectures. 
 NCCL_NET_PLUGIN=ofi
 and NCCL_TUNER_PLUGIN=ofi
, so NCCL uses aws-ofi-nccl. 

 To add EFA to another CUDA 13 SGLang image, build docker/efa.Dockerfile
 on top of it: 
 docker build -f docker/efa.Dockerfile \ 
 --build-arg BASE_IMAGE=lmsysorg/sglang:latest-runtime \ 
 -t sglang:efa docker/ 

 The host still needs the EFA kernel driver. Pass the EFA devices into the container and lift the locked-memory limit: 
 HF_CACHE_DIR = "/path/to/huggingface-cache" 
 docker run -it --gpus all \ 
 --network host \ 
 --ipc=host \ 
 $( for d in /dev/infiniband/uverbs* ; do echo -n "--device $d " ; done) \ 
 --ulimit memlock= -1 \ 
 -v " $HF_CACHE_DIR :/root/.cache/huggingface" \ 
 --env HF_TOKEN \ 
 lmsysorg/sglang:latest-runtime-efa bash 

 Inside the container, fi_info -p efa -t FI_EP_RDM
 lists the EFA devices. 
 ​ Install into your own environment 
 On an x86_64 AWS EFA instance, replace the standard CUDA 13 Mooncake package with the EFA build. The two packages install the same mooncake
 Python module, so do not install them side by side. 
 The following commands replace the package in an SGLang CUDA 13 environment while preserving its existing Python dependencies: 
 python3 -m pip uninstall -y \ 
 mooncake-transfer-engine-cuda13 \ 
 mooncake-transfer-engine-efa-cuda13 
 python3 -m pip install --no-deps \ 
 mooncake-transfer-engine-efa-cuda13==0.3.13.post1 

 export LD_LIBRARY_PATH = "/opt/amazon/efa/lib:/opt/amazon/efa/lib64:${ LD_LIBRARY_PATH :- }" 
 export MOONCAKE_PROTOCOL = efa 

 python3 -c &#x27;from mooncake import engine; assert engine.SUPPORT_EFA&#x27; 
 fi_info -p efa -t FI_EP_RDM 

 The EFA wheel expects a compatible AWS EFA userspace installation on the host and does not bundle libfabric
 or libefa
. The current wheel is available only for x86_64. It is built without Mooncake EP/PG and the NVLink/MNNVL components, so use the standard Mooncake CUDA package for those features. 
 ​ CUDA VMM limitations 
 These limitations apply whenever MOONCAKE_PROTOCOL=efa
, including in the EFA Docker images. 
 The current libfabric EFA provider cannot transfer CUDA VMM-backed buffers. Until libfabric issue #12853 is resolved, apply these settings to both prefill and decode workers: 

 Do not set SGLANG_MOONCAKE_CUSTOM_MEM_POOL
 to NVLINK
 or BAREX
.
 INTRA_NODE_NVLINK
 is supported because it uses the default CUDA allocator. 
 Do not enable expandable_segments:True
 in PYTORCH_CUDA_ALLOC_CONF
 or PYTORCH_ALLOC_CONF
. 
 Post-capture KV sizing is automatically disabled for the EFA protocol because
its KV cache uses CUDA VMM allocations. 
 Do not set MC_FORCE_MNNVL
 or NCCL_MNNVL_ENABLE
; EFA is not an MNNVL transport. 

 SGLang validates the first two constraints at startup to avoid a later fi_read
 or fi_write
 failure from libfabric. 
 ​ IB Device Configuration 
 --disaggregation-ib-device
 supports the following formats when using the Mooncake backend: 

 Shared device list for all GPUs: mlx5_0
 or mlx5_0,mlx5_1

 Per-GPU JSON mapping: {"0": "mlx5_0,mlx5_1", "1": "mlx5_2,mlx5_3"}

 Path to a JSON file containing the same per-GPU mapping 

 Each JSON value uses the same comma-separated device list format as the shared configuration. 
 ​ Usage 
 ​ Llama Single Node 
 python -m sglang.launch_server \ 
 --model-path meta-llama/Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct \ 
 --disaggregation-mode prefill \ 
 --port 30000 \ 
 --disaggregation-ib-device mlx5_roce0 
 python -m sglang.launch_server \ 
 --model-path meta-llama/Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct \ 
 --disaggregation-mode decode \ 
 --port 30001 \ 
 --base-gpu-id 1 \ 
 --disaggregation-ib-device mlx5_roce0 
 python -m sglang_router.launch_router --pd-disaggregation --prefill http://127.0.0.1:30000 --decode http://127.0.0.1:30001 --host 0.0.0.0 --port 8000

 ​ Advanced Configuration 
 PD Disaggregation with Mooncake supports the following environment variables for fine-grained control over system behavior. 
 ​ NVLink Transport Configuration 
 To enable NVLink transport for KV cache transfers with the mooncake backend (recommended for NVL72 deployments), set the following environment variables. Note that auxiliary data transfer will still use TCP as a temporary workaround. 
 Command export SGLANG_MOONCAKE_CUSTOM_MEM_POOL = NVLINK 
 export MC_FORCE_MNNVL = True 

 To utilize Intra-Node NVLink for KV cache transfers with the Mooncake backend (recommended for A100, H20, H100, etc.), set the following environment variables. Please note that auxiliary data still needs to be transferred via TCP. 
 export SGLANG_MOONCAKE_CUSTOM_MEM_POOL = INTRA_NODE_NVLINK 
 export MC_INTRANODE_NVLINK = true 

 The SGLANG_MOONCAKE_CUSTOM_MEM_POOL
 environment variable enables the custom memory pool. Supported values are NVLINK
 (or True
), BAREX
, and INTRA_NODE_NVLINK
. These pools use Mooncake’s CUDA allocators and are CUDA-only; do not set them on Intel XPU. 
 ​ Prefill Server Configuration 
 Variable | Description | Default | 
 SGLANG_DISAGGREGATION_THREAD_POOL_SIZE
 | Controls the total number of worker threads for KVCache transfer operations per TP rank | A dynamic value calculated by int(0.75 * os.cpu_count()) // 8
, which is limited to be larger than 4 and less than 12 to ensure efficiency and prevent thread race conditions | 
 SGLANG_DISAGGREGATION_QUEUE_SIZE
 | Sets the number of parallel transfer queues. KVCache transfer requests from multiple decode instances will be sharded into these queues so that they can share the threads and the transfer bandwidth at the same time. If it is set to 1
, then we transfer requests one by one according to fcfs strategy | 4
 | 
 SGLANG_MOONCAKE_MAX_TRANSFER_BATCH_INDICES
 | Opt-in limit for the number of KV cache indices represented by one synchronous all-layer Mooncake transfer batch. Set it to a positive value to slice larger index arrays into ordered sub-batches before contiguous address ranges are formed. The custom-memory-pool layerwise path is unchanged. | 0
 (disabled) | 
 SGLANG_DISAGGREGATION_BOOTSTRAP_TIMEOUT
 | Timeout (seconds) for receiving destination KV indices during request initialization | 300
 | 
 SGLANG_DISAGGREGATION_BOOTSTRAP_ENTRY_CLEANUP_INTERVAL
 | Interval (seconds) between cleanups of bootstrap entries | 120
 | 

 If a greater mean TTFT is acceptable, you can export SGLANG_DISAGGREGATION_BOOTSTRAP_TIMEOUT=600
 (10 minutes) to relax the timeout condition.
Please be aware that this setting will cause prefill instances to take a longer time to clean up the affected memory resources when a running decode node loses connection. 
 ​ Decode Server Configuration 
 Variable | Description | Default | 
 SGLANG_DISAGGREGATION_HEARTBEAT_INTERVAL
 | Interval (seconds) between health checks to prefill bootstrap servers | 5.0
 | 
 SGLANG_DISAGGREGATION_HEARTBEAT_MAX_FAILURE
 | Consecutive heartbeat failures before marking prefill server offline | 2
 | 
 SGLANG_DISAGGREGATION_WAITING_TIMEOUT
 | Timeout (seconds) for receiving KV Cache after request initialization | 300
 | 

 If a greater mean TTFT is acceptable, you can export SGLANG_DISAGGREGATION_WAITING_TIMEOUT=600
 (10 minutes) to relax the timeout condition. 
 ​ Heterogeneous TP with GPU Staging Buffer 
 When prefill and decode use different tensor parallelism (TP) sizes (e.g., prefill TP=4, decode DP attention with TP=1), the KV cache memory layout differs between the two sides. The GPU staging buffer solves this by gathering KV head slices into a contiguous buffer on the prefill side, performing bulk RDMA transfer, then scattering into the correct KV cache pages on the decode side. This provides 2–5x throughput improvement over the default per-token slice approach at high concurrency and matches homogeneous TP baselines within ~5%. 
 Enable the staging buffer when prefill and decode use different TP sizes with the Mooncake transfer backend. When both sides use the same TP size, staging is automatically bypassed even if enabled. 

 Note: The staging buffer is designed for non-MLA models (e.g. GQA, MHA). MLA models (e.g. DeepSeek-V2/V3) should not enable this flag. 

 Note: The staging buffer also runs on Intel XPU, tested with prefill TP 1 and decode TP 2 on the Mooncake backend. On XPU, Mooncake needs a build with -DUSE_XPU=ON -DUSE_TENT=ON
 and runs with MC_USE_TENT=1
. 

 Note: You can use different attention TP sizes for hybrid sliding-window models (e.g. openai/gpt-oss-120b
) with Mooncake or NIXL. For unequal TP, both backends transfer the sliding-window KV by token and KV-head slice. The staging buffer carries only full-attention KV. Use --pp-size 1
 and leave --enable-unified-memory
 disabled for this configuration. MXFP8 KV cache is not supported for unequal-TP SWA transfer. 

 ​ Environment Variables 
 Variable | Description | Default | 
 SGLANG_DISAGG_STAGING_BUFFER
 | Enable GPU staging buffer for heterogeneous TP KV transfer | False
 | 
 SGLANG_DISAGG_STAGING_POOL_SIZE_MB
 | Decode-side ring buffer pool total size in MB | 4096
 | 

 ​ Usage Example 
 Command # Set staging buffer environment variables on BOTH prefill and decode 
 export SGLANG_DISAGG_STAGING_BUFFER = 1 
 export SGLANG_DISAGG_STAGING_POOL_SIZE_MB = 4096 

 # Prefill with TP=4 
 python -m sglang.launch_server \ 
 --model-path $MODEL_PATH \ 
 --disaggregation-mode prefill \ 
 --port 30000 \ 
 --tp 4 \ 
 --trust-remote-code \ 
 --disaggregation-ib-device mlx5_1,mlx5_2 

 # Decode with TP=1 (or DP attention with effective attention TP=1) 
 python -m sglang.launch_server \ 
 --model-path $MODEL_PATH \ 
 --disaggregation-mode decode \ 
 --port 30001 \ 
 --tp 4 \ 
 --attn-dp-size 4 \ 
 --trust-remote-code \ 
 --disaggregation-ib-device mlx5_3,mlx5_4 

 # Router 
 python -m sglang_router.launch_router \ 
 --pd-disaggregation \ 
 --prefill http://127.0.0.1:30000 \ 
 --decode http://127.0.0.1:30001 \ 
 --host 0.0.0.0 --port 8000 

 ​ NIXL 
 ​ Requirements 
 Install via pip. 
 pip install nixl 

 Or build from source - may be required if you already have UCX installed. 
 git clone https://github.com/ai-dynamo/nixl.git 
 cd nixl 
 pip install . --config-settings=setup-args= "-Ducx_path=/path/to/ucx" 

 On Intel XPU, the PyPI wheel’s bundled UCX has no Level Zero transport. Use the XPU Docker image ( docker/xpu.Dockerfile
), or build UCX with --with-ze
 and NIXL against it; see Intel GPUs (XPU) .

 ​ Advanced Configuration 
 ​ NIXL Backend Selection 
 By default, NIXL uses the UCX backend for KV cache transfers. You can select a different NIXL plugin backend depending on your infrastructure using the environment variable SGLANG_DISAGGREGATION_NIXL_BACKEND
. 
 Example: export SGLANG_DISAGGREGATION_NIXL_BACKEND=LIBFABRIC

 Available backends: UCX (default), LIBFABRIC, or any installed NIXL plugin. 
 Example usage: 
 export SGLANG_DISAGGREGATION_NIXL_BACKEND = LIBFABRIC 
 python -m sglang.launch_server \ 
 --model-path meta-llama/Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct \ 
 --disaggregation-mode prefill \ 
 --disaggregation-transfer-backend nixl \ 
 --port 30000 

 ​ UCX device selection 
 For NVIDIA CUDA and AMD ROCm deployments using NIXL UCX, --disaggregation-ib-device
 accepts a shared HCA list or a per-GPU JSON/file mapping. Mapping keys are process-visible GPU IDs; use HCA names without port suffixes, such as mlx5_0
. 
 A non-empty UCX_NET_DEVICES
 takes precedence over this CLI option. SGLang leaves the environment variable unchanged and skips the CLI mapping, including per-GPU mappings, with a warning. This preserves interfaces such as lo
 in an existing configuration like rdma0:1,rdma4:1,lo
, which some RoCE hosts need for NIXL’s self-endpoint. 
 To use per-GPU CLI selection, unset UCX_NET_DEVICES
; keep it configured on hosts that require the loopback entry. An explicit device_list
 in SGLANG_DISAGGREGATION_NIXL_BACKEND_PARAMS
 is left unchanged and still overrides UCX_NET_DEVICES
; do not set it if you rely on the environment’s complete device list. 
 ​ ASCEND 
 ​ Usage 
 Use ascend backend with memfabric_hybrid and ASCEND_MF_STORE_URL being set 
 Command pip install memfabric-hybrid== 1.0.0 
 export ASCEND_MF_STORE_URL = "tcp://xxx.xx.xxx.xxx:xxxx" 

 Use mooncake backend, more details can be found in mooncake section. 
 export ENABLE_ASCEND_TRANSFER_WITH_MOONCAKE = true 

 ASCEND_NPU_PHY_ID need to be set in container env 
 export ASCEND_NPU_PHY_ID = xxx 

 ​ MIMO Single Node 
 ​ prefill 
 # high performance cpu 
 echo performance | tee /sys/devices/system/cpu/cpu * /cpufreq/scaling_governor 
 sysctl -w vm.swappiness= 0 
 sysctl -w kernel.numa_balancing= 0 
 sysctl -w kernel.sched_migration_cost_ns= 50000 
 # bind cpu 
 export SGLANG_SET_CPU_AFFINITY = 1 

 unset https_proxy

 unset HTTP_PROXY

 # cann

 export HCCL_OP_EXPANSION_MODE = AIV 
 export HCCL_SOCKET_IFNAME = lo 
 export GLOO_SOCKET_IFNAME = lo

_jsxs(_Fragment,{children:[_jsx(Heading,{level:`2`,id:`why-and-what-is-pd-disaggregation`,children:`Why and What is PD Disaggregation?`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`Large Language Model (LLM) inference comprises two distinct phases: `,_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`Prefill`}),` and `,_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`Decode`}),`. The Prefill phase is computation-intensive, processing the entire input sequence, while the Decode phase is memory-intensive, managing the Key-Value (KV) cache for token generation. Traditionally, these phases are handled within a unified engine, where combined scheduling of prefill and decode batches introduces inefficiencies. To address these challenges, we introduce `,_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`Prefill and Decoding (PD) Disaggregation`}),` in SGLang.`]}),`\n`,_jsx(Heading,{level:`3`,id:`issues-with-unified-scheduling`,children:`Issues with Unified Scheduling`}),`\n`,_jsx(_components.p,{children:`The conventional unified engine, which processes prefill and decode batches together, results in two significant problems:`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.ol,{children:[`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`Prefill Interruption`}),`: Incoming prefill batches frequently interrupt ongoing decode batches, causing substantial delays in token generation.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`DP Attention Imbalance`}),`: In data-parallel (DP) attention, one DP worker may process a prefill batch while another handles a decode batch simultaneously, leading to increased decode latency.`]}),`\n`]}),`\n`,_jsx(_components.p,{children:`PD Disaggregation resolves these by separating the two stages, enabling tailored optimizations for each.`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`For the design details, please refer to `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`https://docs.google.com/document/d/1rQXJwKd5b9b1aOzLh98mnyMhBMhlxXA5ATZTHoQrwvc/edit?tab=t.0`,children:`link`}),`.`]}),`\n`,_jsx(_components.p,{children:`Currently, we support Mooncake and NIXL as the transfer engine.`}),`\n`,_jsx(Heading,{level:`2`,id:`decode-allocation-policy`,children:`Decode allocation policy`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`By default, decode reserves each request’s destination KV slots before prefill\nfinishes. Long prefills can hold these slots while decode waits for the KV data.\nTo delay this reservation, set the following flag on `,_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`both prefill and decode`}),`:`]}),`\n`,_jsx(CodeBlock,{filename:``,numberOfLines:`1`,language:`shellscript`,children:_jsx(_components.pre,{className:`shiki shiki-themes github-light-default dark-plus`,style:{backgroundColor:`#ffffff`,\"--shiki-dark-bg\":`#0B0C0E`,color:`#1f2328`,\"--shiki-dark\":`#D4D4D4`},language:`shellscript`,children:_jsxs(_components.code,{language:`shellscript`,numberOfLines:`1`,children:[_jsxs(_components.span,{className:`line`,children:[_jsx(_components.span,{style:{color:`#953800`,\"--shiki-dark\":`#DCDCAA`},children:`--disaggregation-decode-allocation-policy`}),_jsx(_components.span,{style:{color:`#0A3069`,\"--shiki-dark\":`#CE9178`},children:` prefill_complete`})]}),`\n`]})})}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`With `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`prefill_complete`}),`, prefill computes without waiting for destination slots,\nthen signals readiness after accepting its final prefill result. Decode reserves\nslots and starts the existing KV transfer handshake only after that signal.\nThis changes per-request KV reservation, not the size or creation of the global\nKV pool. The default `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`early`}),` policy preserves the existing behavior.`]}),`\n`,_jsx(_components.p,{children:`Deferring allocation can help when long prefills compete with active decode\nrequests for destination KV capacity. It also retains KV on prefill longer and\nremoves overlap between chunked prefill and KV transfer. Compare both policies\nfor your workload; this option is not a general throughput improvement.`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`This initial implementation requires Mooncake (RDMA or `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`mooncake_tcp`}),`), the Python\nbootstrap registry, and attention TP/CP and pipeline sizes of one. Attention DP\nis allowed when each DP rank has attention TP size one. Prefill automatically\nresolves `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`--optimistic-prefill-attempts`}),` from zero to one; configurations that\ndisable optimistic prefill after model validation are rejected.\nChunked prefill does not yield just because decode has not admitted the request.\nCompleted requests retain their KV in the existing optimistic-prefill inflight\nqueue until decode supplies destination indices; readiness alone never starts\na KV write.\nAfter a successful transfer, prefill clears the room’s readiness and transfer\nstate before notifying decode of success. Retraction recovery can then reuse\nthe same bootstrap room. This notification waits for worker and scheduler\ncleanup; failure and cancellation records remain until their timeout expires.\nRetries remain eligible for prefill after the optimistic attempt budget is\nexhausted and remain subject to the existing cancellation and timeout handling.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`On prefill, `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`SGLANG_DISAGGREGATION_BOOTSTRAP_TIMEOUT`}),` starts after the final\nprefill result, giving decode admission its own budget. On decode,\n`,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`SGLANG_DISAGGREGATION_WAITING_TIMEOUT`}),` bounds the wait for prefill completion,\nthen starts afresh for admission and again when destination indices are sent\nfor transfer.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`The option does not support staging buffers, runtime PD role switching,\nencoder disaggregation, decoupled speculation, HiSparse, decode radix cache, decode\nHiCache, or non-DSA auxiliary state pools. Prefill HiCache is limited to L2 with\n`,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`write_back`}),` and no storage backend. Unsupported configurations fail validation.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`All communicating workers must use the same revision and enable the same policy. This\nversion of the decode worker rejects policy or peer-layout mismatches before\ndestination registration. Older decode workers do not enforce this check. Keep\n`,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`early`}),` on both sides until every worker has been upgraded, then enable the new\npolicy on both sides.`]}),`\n`,_jsx(Heading,{level:`2`,id:`profiling-in-pd-disaggregation-mode`,children:`Profiling in PD Disaggregation Mode`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`When you need to profile prefill or decode workers in PD disaggregation mode, please refer to the `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/docs/developer_guide/benchmark_and_profiling#profile-in-pd-disaggregation-mode`,children:`Profile In PD Disaggregation Mode`}),` section in the Benchmark and Profiling guide. Their process-local Responses stores cannot provide coherent shared state: both legs need the same conversation history to reconstruct the prompt, and prefill’s stored response is not available to decode. decode use different tensor parallelism (TP) sizes (e.g., prefill TP=4, decode DP attention with TP=1), the KV cache memory layout differs between the two sides. The `,_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`GPU staging buffer`}),` solves this by gathering KV head slices into a contiguous buffer on the prefill side, performing bulk RDMA transfer, then scattering into the correct KV cache pages on the decode side. This provides `,_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`2–5x throughput improvement`}),` over the default per-token slice approach at high concurrency and matches homogeneous TP baselines within ~5%.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`Enable the staging buffer when prefill and decode use `,_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`different TP sizes`}),` with the `,_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`Mooncake`}),` transfer backend. When both sides use the same TP size, staging is automatically bypassed even if enabled.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.blockquote,{children:[`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`Note:`}),` The staging buffer is designed for non-MLA models (e.g. GQA, MHA). MLA models (e.g. DeepSeek-V2/V3) should not enable this flag.`]}),`\n`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.blockquote,{children:[`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`Note:`}),` The staging buffer also runs on Intel XPU, tested with prefill TP 1 and decode TP 2 on the Mooncake backend. For unequal TP, both backends transfer the sliding-window KV by token and KV-head slice. The staging buffer carries only full-attention KV. Use `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`--pp-size 1`}),` and leave `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`--enable-unified-memory`}),` disabled for this configuration.
